# Backlog — every open suggestion, so nothing is lost between sessions

The running list of the user's suggestions and deferred work. Each item has an
id, the round it came from (DECISIONS.md has the user's words), a size
(S ≈ hours, M ≈ half a day, L ≈ a day+) and a status. When an item ships,
move it to **Done** with the commit; when the user changes their mind, note
it here. Agents: read this before starting work and keep it current in the
same commit as the change.

Order agreed (round 10): A1–A5, A8, A12, A13 → A10, A14, A11 → A9 → A7 → A15 render slopes → A6 + A15 sediment layer. Answers: A15 render first then sediment; A7 the player taps the lightning; A9 on the title screen, default Medium.

Status: `won't do` (user declined) · `open` · `next` (agreed, queued) · `wip` · `asked` (waiting on the
user) · `deferred` (agreed for later).

Then: "I think it had a function of the inflow that was not just linear interpolation so that shallow bits dry first from what I recall.") **A river's depths as a per-column function of one inflow float on the atlas, no simulation**: one float per river (or per basin / region) on the atlas; each column's depth a function of it, not a linear blend between a low-water and a high-water steady state — shallow columns dry first as the flow drops (e.g. each column stores the flow at which it wets, plus a stage curve above that). 0 a dry bed, high a flood. Missing: the flood side (the shift is capped at 0.28 over the reference and stays in the channel: no flood plain, lakes over their spill), a per-column wetting flow / stage curve where one level shift isn't enough (a braided bed, pools that hold water after the riffles dry), the float living on the atlas per river rather than as the window's wetness field, and the window's edge taking that level (UC-6's edge reads riverHere, so it follows `wet` already). ## Round 115 (2026-10-30, session 5, `places`) — caching by section, travel on the atlas, the camera's view (DECISIONS round 115)

Streaming is good enough for now (places 3c850b9); these are for when gameplay and the finished generator are built.

| id | item | size | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| RC1 | Load a section ahead and keep it: gameplay keeps the player inside a section of the map; its tiles (and regions) are made before they arrive there and stay resident on the GPU while they are in it — the tile atlas gets a pinned set per section beside its least-recently-drawn pool, sized from the section | M | deferred |
| RC2 | Travel on the atlas map: the player goes from place to place on the atlas map; the destination is precached while the travel is animated on the map, and the place opens when it is resident | M | deferred |
| RC3 | En route: events on the way, new POI generated along the path, or a fade between pretty views along the road and the map — the travel covers the loading and is fun in itself | M | deferred |
| RC4 | Generation partitions the map into regions with natural barriers on their borders (cliffs, water, ridges), so play is coherent within a region and the cache loads by region (RC1); the region graph is shared by map design (rooms, key and door) and streaming | L | deferred |
| RC5 | What the tile atlas keeps today, to revisit with RC1: a fixed GPU budget of slots (desktop 384 / 144 MB, phone 104 / 39 MB), least recently drawn out (never a tile the last frame drew); the planner asks for the footprint plus look-ahead along the motion; nothing is kept for what is likely to come back besides recency; tiles are keyed by the view's turn (a turn bakes all of them again) and live on the GPU only (a reload makes them again; WV6) | S | open |
| OC1 | The camera's view kept clear: the camera slides over the heightmap toward the ground at the centre of the screen, which is always visible (Camera.focus, round 114); what blocks the view of it is dithered by a rule for how much of the nearby surface a thing may hide, by its height and maybe its layers — small things like rocks may, a cliff or spire hiding the ground may not. Replaces the tiles' eye-side cut (FOCUS_HOLE) and the rings' pressing; one rule for the window, the tiles and the rings; uses the Dither contract (api/cutaway.ts) | M | open |
## Round 115 (2026-10-30, `tilecal`) — the tiles calibrated to the device and the screen (DECISIONS round 115)

The user: the desktop on their wide monitor "does not load all the tiles"; investigate on the PC whether it's settings,
and if so make the runtime calibrate to the hardware.

| id | item | size | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| CAL1 | Reproduce on wide screens (2560 x 1080, 3440 x 1440, 5120 x 1440, dpr 1) and name the cause: the zoom floor from a fixed fill rate (2 tiles a frame; measured here 1.1-1.2) and an atlas capped at 128 MB (459 slots for every screen past ~2000 x 1000) — parked views load whole inside the floor on this iGPU; the floor rose 2.57 → 5.2 with the screen | S | done (`tilecal`): tile gate `--screens uw2560,uw3440,uw5120`, scripts/fillprobe.mjs |
| CAL2 | The runtime calibrates: the land's fill rate measured (tiles/s while it's the bottleneck, at rest, holes first), the floor by the screen's own regions (area and perimeter) in 5 s at that rate, the atlas grown in place (rows added, slots kept) when it's the floor's bound, within a memory budget; stats in TileCache.stats / cal.stats, the gate's rows and the pan bench | M | done (`tilecal`): floors 1600 x 900 2.57 → 1.95, 5120 x 1440 5.2 → 4.27 (60 Hz gate), phone 2.78 → 2.78; parked 0 out of detail everywhere |
| CAL3 | The atlas's memory budget (was up to 512 MB off a phone, 128 MB on one) — the user: "Why limit to 512 and 128MB both should use as much as we need that the hardware supports." No policy caps: grown to need, bounded by the device's texture side (the adapter's maxTextureDimension2D asked for) and allocations that succeed (an 'out-of-memory' error scope; a failure keeps the atlas and is the ceiling, `cal.stats.ceilingBytes`); the phone the same | S | done (`tilecal`) |

## Round 112 (2026-10-29, session 5, `physfix2`) — the playground's physics on the phone (DECISIONS round 112)

The user, verbatim: "Bugs from physics playing: The concrete just immediately vanished when any blocks are deleted. The bridge on the arch also just vanished when I damaged one side and the other broke instantly. The tower just settled at a leaning angle. The thin stone protrusion survived a fall and collision. Is sand expected to always fall from the mountains." Then, on the sand: "I think it's just the wind blowing dust visual."; on the
protrusion: "Maybe we can find thin points with a quick JFA based medial axis to find thin areas where mass on either side
is large?"

| id | item | size | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PF1 | "The concrete just immediately vanished when any blocks are deleted": what's built (concrete, brick, metal) freed by the rock judge is a rigid body (it takes what hangs free whatever it's made of), and the rigid body is drawn by the rock mesh — the field mesh's mesher, which draws no built material (meshregion.wgsl fieldMat: what's built is prisms). A concrete piece was a body with an empty mesh and no cubes (it had a slot), and at rest External cells nothing drew. Now the rock mesh is made of what the field mesh draws (Integrity.allocRock), the rest of a body's cells are drawn as its voxels (RockBodies.packInto by cell), moving and at rest (the resting entry's voxels: rockbodies.ts restingVoxels); a body with no mesh rests too, as its voxels (not written back as a heap). world/materials.ts fieldMeshed mirrors fieldMat (terrain.ts's copy had drifted) | M | done (this commit) |
| PF2 | "The bridge on the arch also just vanished when I damaged one side and the other broke instantly": the build station's bridge (a concrete deck on brick pillars) is PF1 — the deck and the pillar it took went invisible. The rock station's arch did not vanish on places (physfix's fix holds): smashing a foot frees the leg with half the span — the span snaps at its middle under the hanging leg (a load break, the right call: a 12-cell rock cantilever 3 thick doesn't hold its leg), the leg drops the hole's height and stands. It struck the ground at 9.2 cells/s (its break speed 8.7) and survived because a tipping piece had 1 s of grace: now a body's grace ends once it has been off the ground 0.15 s (RockBody.airborne): the leg breaks where it lands | S | done (this commit) |
| PF3 | "The tower just settled at a leaning angle": a body whose centre of mass is past what it touches now topples. The contacts' ground damping (rigid.contactDamp 1.5/s) took its spin out each substep while it pivoted on its edge — the 1 500-cell pillar's topple crawled (2° to 30° in 10 s) and, slow enough (under rigid.restV, restW), counted as at rest, leaning. Now RockBody.balanced (its centre of mass over the hull of its contact points, within rigid.balanceGive): unbalanced, no ground damping and not at rest. The mass-scaled overlap doesn't prop it (the support is the footprint it sank with). The tall pillar now falls 2° to 30° in 4 s and on down onto the ground; leaning on a neighbour's cap (a real support) it stands | S | done (this commit) |
| PF4 | "The thin stone protrusion survived a fall and collision": a break was judged against the whole body's mass (RB.breakSpeed × √strength × (cells/200)^−sizeK) and split it by random seeds, so a thin part never snapped on its own. Now each body's necks (findNecks, at spawn): the distance transform of its cells (a breadth-first sweep on the lattice — the JFA's output, on the CPU because the bodies are), the medial axis (cells midway across it along one of the lattice's four axes), each thin axis cell tried: a ball of its thickness out, the rest in two sides of rigid.neckMin cells or more. A hit snaps the worst neck when (mass beyond it from the point hit) × speed / (its cross-section) passes rigid.neckStrength × strength — in two, no rubble. fracture.ts NECK_R ("neck reach") is not a thinness test (how far the judge follows a piece above a cut before calling it keyed into the land); the standing rock's thin sections are the judge's joints (BEND h², SHEAR h, buckle) | M | done (this commit) |
| PF5 | "Is sand expected to always fall from the mountains": no. The range's peaks are soil the wind can't lift (lift 0 measured) — what fell was the sliding grains: a chunk falls asleep 8 s after its event, the sediment stops, and the air texture it wrote for the particles (what slides off each column) kept its last tick's flow: 1 340 columns streamed grains for ever over ground that moved 0.00 in 90 s. Now nothing streams off a sleeping column. And (the user: "I think it's just the wind blowing dust visual") where nothing is lifted (asleep, or the wind's work baked) dust is shown by the wind's strength over the loose material there (its lift, airborne share), modest (dust.shown), carrying nothing — not one tick's lift at full strength for ever | S | done (this commit) |
| PF6 | A slope that falls asleep mid-slump stays as it froze (steeper than its repose) until something wakes it: sleep keeps a chunk awake past its time only while its water changes (?settletol, waterChange); its loose ground moving should hold it too (a per-chunk ground-change from the sediment: it has no storage binding to spare) | S | open |

| id | item | size | status |
|---|---|---|---|

| TF4 | The disk hitch: the pool's uploads split across frames, 0.5 MB a frame on a phone (a region's record is 1.7 MB: 4 frames); the desktop can't show Mali's writeBuffer stall | S | done (`tilefast`), **the phone to confirm** (disk vs generated rows) |
| TF5 | The pop-in at zoom 5 "behind": a tall window-baked tile just past the window on the eye's side is left out (it would rise into the line to the focus) and the rings draw it flat. Options: draw it with the window's cut (fsTile: the cut test on the texel's position from its depth), or press it down as the rings do | M | done (`tilefix2`, the user's option (a)): fsTile cuts what rises into the line from the eye to the focus (the rings' rule); no tile left out |
| TF6 | After a zoom-out the screen's tiles are the closer zoom's whole slots, all drawn, so nothing can be evicted (noSlot for seconds): they're made again at the view's size, a region at a time on the land's lane. Faster: a GPU downsample of a drawn tile into a smaller part (no land made again) | M | open (remakes done; the downsample open) |
| TF7 | The land's latency where the streamer's lane is busy (zoom 14, 80 c/s: ~7 frames — the tiles get half the turns) and with 6 regions in flight at a wide zoom (24 frames at 2.2, 80 c/s): the pacer's share by urgency (the screen's wait) rather than half | S | open |

## Round 110 (2026-10-29, session 5, `physfix`) — the rigid rock's four faults in the playground (DECISIONS round 110)

The user, after playing the playground, verbatim: "Destroying the base of an arch on either side causes the bridge
connecting the sides to just instantly disappear. When you destroy material around a rock it pushes up out of the
earth before becoming a rigidbody. I think we want meshes to intersect. We don't want collision to kick in until they
are visibly intersecting for large objects. When things come to rest they turn to dust instead of staying as meshes
as we discussed." Then: "For physics maybe a tunable parameter for how much objects can interpenetrate maybe as a
function of mass?" Within the CPU rigid rock (round 106's design; the GPU solver, AR4, later).

| id | item | size | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PX1 | "Destroying the base of an arch on either side causes the bridge connecting the sides to just instantly disappear": the span falls as a body and stays one. Root: the half that gave fell onto its stub and came to rest in a few seconds, and a rested body was written back into the columns cell by cell, each cell dropped down its own column (Integrity.settleRigid, "a heap settles column by column") — the span over air collapsed to a heap on the ground in one frame. Now it stays its mesh (PX4), and a joint that gives under load hinges at its lower edge (fracture.ts hingeOf: the span topples about the other base) | S | done (this commit) |
| PX2 | "When you destroy material around a rock it pushes up out of the earth before becoming a rigidbody": a body freed in place is not lifted. Roots: its collision points were a cube's corners, which lie in the next hex columns over (a cell is 0.5 to its faces, the cube's corners 0.65 out), so a body freed beside ground began inside it and was pushed up out of it at half the overlap a substep; and a speculative contact 0.5 above the ground. Now the hex prism's 12 corners; the overlap it's freed with allowed (RockBody.allow, only shrinking); a push-out never faster than rigid.pushV and never past the slop + erodeDepth (deeper, the ground there crumbles: rigid.erodeDepth, erodeMax); a break's pieces thrown sideways only, never faster up than the body was | M | done (this commit) |
| PX3 | "We don't want collision to kick in until they are visibly intersecting for large objects" and "a tunable parameter for how much objects can interpenetrate maybe as a function of mass?": the overlap a contact allows is rigid.slop + min(rigid.slopCap, rigid.slopK · mass^rigid.slopP) (defaults 0.03 + min(0.8, 0.065 · m^0.35): a 30-cell piece 0.23, a 260-cell span 0.48, a 1 500-cell pillar 0.83). A pair (a body on a resting rock) meets at the lighter one's; the ground counts as heaviest. The probe measures two rings of columns deep (to 2 cells), past the largest allowance | S | done (this commit) |
| PX4 | "When things come to rest they turn to dust instead of staying as meshes as we discussed": a body at rest stays its mesh, asleep (the resting rock: External cells under it, round 99's MI4), with the rigid rock everywhere — not only `?meshinst=1`. One cut or hanging free wakes as a body where it lies, its own mesh (MI5); cut, it loses the cells dug out of it (meshed again behind dust). Baked into the ground only when out of room (rigid.restMax, 16; the rock mesh's slots), leaving the window, or asked (Integrity.bakeResting: between runs) — each behind a burst of dust | M | done (this commit) |
| PX5 | Between runs: call Integrity.bakeResting when a run ends (no run-end hook in the level scene yet; the resting rock is saved with its region meanwhile: RG7) | S | open |
| PX6 | A body against another body (two awake ones) is still not simulated: each meets only the ground and the resting rock. The GPU solver (AR4) | M | done (solver, the node solver: every awake body meets the others — point in the other's node blocks, both ways by contact mass; stacking holds) |

| id | item | size | status |
|---|---|---|---|

| SH4 | A loaded record's body uploaded into the pool as it came (no split into layers/bufs/texs and back); `rs.load` the main thread's decode only (it timed the worker's wait); a readback's hash and encode one region a task, a few ms a frame (`Streamer.later`), the stage read in place | S | done (this commit) |
| SH5 | The pool's victim: the region farthest from the window's middle a second of the camera's motion on (BN28) | S | done (this commit) |
| SH6 | Left after SH1–SH5: the frames over 33 ms at 60 cells/s (5 generated, 9 pool, 11 disk in 10 s) are gpu-held 60–90 ms frames mostly not at a slide (the GPU's own frame 20–40 ms: structure, draw, agents) — simscale's and tilecache's ground; the shift still copies every per-column buffer twice through the scratch (~400 MB of copies a slide at window 512); with CPU throttle 4x the generated pan's p99 is 138 ms (gpu-held, gc), the base not measured throttled; `?bench=pan` before/after, its speed ramp and the phone not run yet (GPU time) | M | open |

Desktop: the copies a slide ~400 → ~130 MB at window 512, the slide's GPU 13 ms (max 20) at 512, 5 ms at 256 | S | done (this commit) |
| SH8 | Uploads into the pool at most 2 MB a frame (`?uploadmb=`), one region at least | S | done (this commit) |
| SH9 | The slide's CPU part (desktop, CPU throttle 4x, window 256: 26 ms at most, 9 on average): the freeze's CPU (saveItem, things) ~7 ms, the window's CPU grids, the camera, the events, onShift and the mesh's windowMoved ~9, the level's onSlide (cb1: integrity.slid's re-projection, the surface cache) ~5, a region's write up to 12. Spread it: the CPU grids moved by offset not copied, the freeze's CPU parts taken a frame ahead, the writes over frames | M | open |

The warp back is still exact: the 4 regions play changed came back from disk, 4 of 4 identical by hash (`n 4, bad 0, loaded 4`); the untouched ones are made again. Half rate isn't cleanly stable: its wind carry scales with the time step while its slumping, smoothing and piping are per pass, so per-pass transport would double against the same smoothing, and doubling the smoothing overshoots (six neighbours each taking 0.2). Open items: (a) coast gets ~10 sites with the defaults (4 streets, or 2 under 30°), so its threshold needs the user's eye; (b) the line sides of a block that crosses a circle aren't clipped (only its street corners are); (c) a street passing right through a circle is dropped, not split; (d) overlapping sites keep the bigger one rather than merging; (e) the full rebuild is about 1.2 ms (trace +0.08 ms, mesh +0.04 ms). | S | open |
| ST7 | Street lab m3f is done in this branch: cross streets march to the next line or a ridge when no seed pairs; gap fill (empty land labelled into regions, split recursively, leaves as blocks); roads at a big block's first two splits. Open items: (a) the full rebuild is now 1.45–1.6 ms; the gap fill (~0.27 ms) is mostly fixed-size work (12 labelling rounds, 1.1 M node words cleared, nodes run per tile × 16), so compacting regions to their roots is the next cut; (b) gap blocks are oriented boxes from moments, shrunk to clear their neighbours, so they read boxier than the field blocks and leave margins; (c) the labelling may not converge in 12 rounds for long snaking regions (a region split in two is harmless); (d) the split roads span the region's spread, so they can poke past its edge; (e) ridge-ended strips build blocks back to back with no road at the ridge. | M | open |
| ST8 | Street lab m3g is done in this branch: the mask is blurred (not the distance), cross streets are grown line by line (Jobard–Lefer: march, stop at a T if too near, wake a seed where they spread), and only the top-K landmark sites are kept. All are defaults, with the old ways kept as radio options. Open items: (a) the full rebuild is 1.67–1.8 ms (trace 0.66–0.75 ms with 8 growth lines × 4 dispatches; gap fill 0.3 ms); (b) gap fill is still needed (river lots drop from 418 to 313 without it), mostly where the next-along link fails and strips end at a ridge or the town edge; (c) a spine road at the creases isn't drawn; (d) the stop/wake tests read one id per grid cell, so close pairs hidden behind a cell's minimum are missed; (e) lines above 8 levels never grow. | M | open |
| ST9 | Street lab m3h is done in this branch: supercells (a jittered grid, 4 block lengths apart) decide the city's limit (whole blocks, so the edge runs along streets), its empty districts (majors and arterials kept) and its growth (each block's key, from its centre, kept in its meta on the GPU; a growth slider); a district tint toggle. Open items: (a) supercells are a jittered grid plus a 3 × 3 nearest search, not a JFA, and the centres aren't seeds (a centre's cell is never empty); (b) the growth key is a float in each block's meta (word 9), not an integer rank; (c) an arterial is a hash (one street in three), not a street that actually runs far; tendrils past the limit read as stray stubs, so they're off; (d) the full rebuild is about 1.9 ms with everything on (noisy); (e) at growth 0.3 only the centres' cells show. | S | open |

## TM — town moods (the user, 2026-11-10; not built — the season's opportunity effects to come)

The user, verbatim: "military checkpoints in the city to make it look more authoritarian in response to civil
opportunities or initiate a curfew, or a parade, or to have the dead being collected in the street because of plague,
or to have the characters become undead and work endlessly and have homes go into disrepair. That plus lighting and
weather effects; storms, fog and then the unit graphics push so merchant carts and markets and zombies and people look
more different … Prosperous, ruined, destroyed etc. different banners and lighting all will make this truly
expressive." Pointer: docs/design/SEASON_LOOP.md "Town moods".

## AG — the building generator (archgen, 2026-11-11; docs/design/ARCHGEN.md)

- AG1 The lab: archlab.html over captured Muru lots; parts expanded in the vertex shader; orbit camera at play pitch.
- AG2 Massing: body, setbacks, wings, roofs (flat/shed/gable/hip/pyramid), overhangs, corner towers.
- AG3 Procedural materials and painted windows/doors in the fragment shader; detail fades on the phone.
- AG4 Style profiles (homes, port warehouses, keep and walls) as data; districtStyle word (Track A's buffer).
- AG5 Ornament parts: plinths, cornices, parapets/crenels, chimneys, spires.
- AG6 Wire-in: TownBuildingsSink lifecycle, drawBuildings flag, generation sliced over frames, game lighting/shadows.
- AG7 Phone toggle bench case (town generation + draw on/off).
- AG8 Construction-shader inputs agreed (lot index, local height, floor, roof flag, eave height).
- AG9 Later: cutaway LOD (thick walls, slabs), the gesture editor, route anchors on walkable parts, height rule moved
  into the style table (Track A's offer), other families (node graph, SDF, irregular grid).
- AG10 Landmark sites (round plots, records from counts[3]): the generator to make them (a ring building with a
  central tower, DESIGN §18); citygen still draws them meanwhile (Track A asked for them to be covered).
- AG11 Brighter albedo under the game's lighting (it reads darker and more muted than the lab), and more than one
  style in a town's core (Muru's core hashed to one style).
- AG12 (Track A review of 14ff29a1, approved pending the user's sign-off on the look) — the reach cut-off outline
  sits at the record's top: move it to the generated roof later; districtStyle: `setDistrictStyle(district, word)` on
  CityGen/FieldTown, saved in TownLog beside `purposes`, a WGSL accessor snippet like townplace.wgsl; confirm "no
  pacing" with the phone toggle bench (towns stream back to back) before relying on it (AG7).
- AG13 Roofs, towers, eaves and jetties go past footprint × record height (told Track A). **A gate on making archgen
  the default** (Track A, DESIGN §3/§9/§10: the world sees buildings only through their claims). Track A leans to (a):
  the generator's GPU pass writes each record's envelope before citygen's claim pass — eave height, a roof/tower top
  (a new record word), the overhang as an outset — and citygen's claim reads it; (b) the generator takes building
  lots' claims over per record. Thin features under ~0.5 (spires, chimneys, overhangs) may stay unclaimed. Propose
  it in ARCHGEN.md with the height rule once the user has judged the look.
- AG14 Vertex budget on the phone: ~0.76 M vertices for Muru without the built windows (roofs 0.34 M, cones 0.2 M):
  a phone bench (AG7) decides; then fewer courses on the phone or far away (a roof kind of 6 courses), cones of 10
  segments, dead boxes culled by splitting the window kind (frame/pane vs shutters/boxes).
- AG15 Round 2 gaps: hip seams (courses of a slope and its hip end don't meet: dark fill under them; hip and ridge
  caps to come), timber framing painted not in relief, lot walls doubled along shared edges, the porch/stair door not
  moved to the stair's head, no arcades/loggias or crow-stepped gables yet (the references).
- AG16 (built per lot on the GPU, see DEVLOG; per-block grids with 3- and 5-valent vertices, bridges and stacked terraces across lots still open) An irregular-grid family (Townscaper) for shanties and larger structures: the existing
  structures/tiles/* grids and modules.ts kit (warped modules on irregular quads) behind the same parts/output
  contract; stacked terraces, arches, stilts, bridges, narrow coloured houses along curving rows.
- AG17 Lots don't know water: docks and water wheels can't check they're by water (a water bit or distance in the
  record, or the generator sampling the town's water mask).
- AG18 (the user, 2026-11-11: "good shading is well solved … we should be using a stronger lighting model") One
  standard lighting model for the lab and the game: diffuse + specular with roughness per material, sky light, AO
  (SSAO/GTAO on PC, per-part on the phone), tone mapping and exposure; today light.wgsl is Lambert + hemisphere + a
  fake fill with no specular or tone mapping, and the lab lights with its own model. Shared by every system: who owns
  it (here or the art_improvements session) is the user's call.
  (archgen-ground, 2026-11-11: started — light.wgsl lightSurf: GGX sun highlight by roughness, Fresnel sky, AO on the
  ambient; the terrain and the buildings use it, ?light=old the old. Still open: tone mapping and exposure (a global
  post pass), SSAO, the lab's own lighting, the prisms/boxes/tiles' fsTile still on lighting().)
- AG21 (archgen-ground) Ground textures, next steps: a height channel per set (today the colour's brightness stands in
  for it in the grass blend); hex-tile stochastic sampling to hide the repeat when zoomed out; the town mesh's dirt,
  cobble and grass (bodies.wgsl groundLook, Track A's growing-road dirt REV_DIRT_S) from the same sets; KTX2/BC
  compression (20 sets × 2 arrays at 1k is ~220 MB of RGBA8 with mips); the tiles' bake keeps no roughness (fsTile
  lights a frozen tile without a highlight); a stylized look (the user: "Dota 2 does have very pretty land and grass
  and plants") — painted sets or a stronger palette pull on the photo sets.
  (2026-11-12, the user on the phone: "The texture is basically pointless then at this scale" — at play zoom a cell
  is ~4 px, the 2–3 m repeat mips to its mean. Ground textures off on the phone; maybe back for the closest fixed zoom
  step only, or as macro variation several cells across.)
- AG22 (2026-11-12, the phone) Roofs drawn as long spikes running up the screen to a few far points (a vertex very high:
  a finite height, not the NaN the a6745c06 guard catches). Desktop dump clean (?archdump=1, 0 odd parts); waiting on
  the phone's dump to compare word for word. (Found: the grid's run-time-modulo corner index on the Mali; fixed
  the same day, 524c6b08 — see DEVLOG. Confirmed on the phone: its next dump, 667 lots, 0 stretched parts. Done.)
- AG23a done (the user, 2026-11-12: "fix the triangles on the irregular grid first ... They look terrible"): no
  triangle terraces (a lone triangle's three quad cells stand on their own); a cell with three corners past a street,
  or pulled into a straight line, is dropped. The shared world grid below is still open.
- AG23 (the user, 2026-11-12) One irregular grid shared by every lot, not one per lot: "Can't disjoint lots share the
  same irregular grid? ... then you could even build features like bridges over the city roads." Quads only ("The
  irregular tiles here also gave triangle tiles and do not follow the quad based design I expect"). Lanes inside a
  cluster are grid cells; buildings never over a road at ground level ("the building seem to overlap roads ... in a
  most displeasing way"); a road cell may carry a bridge from the first storey up. Plan: a world-fixed triangle lattice,
  each triangle split into 3 quads (all quads, no global pairing), corners jittered by a hash of their lattice point so
  neighbours agree; lots claim the cells inside their parcel. Covers the grid rework (shared heights, per-corner roofs).
- AG24 (the user, 2026-11-12) The phone draws the static world from baked tiles, Age of Empires 2 style: "if we don't
  allow camera rotation can't we essentially render the whole ground and buildings in only one frame as a static lit
  sprite or as tiles? ... it lets us bake the anti aliasing detail at super resolution into the map. Then we need only
  redraw animated layers". Ground and buildings baked (2× then down) as albedo, normal, depth layers into the tile
  cache (render/tilecache.ts already bakes terrain per region and turn), lit each frame from the layers, one baked
  shadow render; re-bake on change; animated layers drawn live. Wind: a per-pixel sway channel the lighting pass
  offsets its lookup by ("Bending sprites with a few control points in a grid to warp fragments"). Gate: the phone
  bench (frame, pan, memory).
- AG27 (the user, 2026-11-12: "please log hacks to the backlog as things to fix. We can always defer them") The
  hacks and limits of the archgen grid / look work, each to fix:
  - Grid lots: lotAt() loops every lot of the town, five times a cell (and in tgEmit) — a per-chunk lot index; the 3 m
    reach for the slivers between lots (lets cells reach a little past the coast); outerStreet's 4 m probe across a
    street (a wide street or odd parcel can fool it); street fronts (doors, gables) still by nearness (1.6 m) to the
    lot's own street line, not to the open cells; the lots' 96-part budget still holds grid cells (a per-chunk pool);
    streets and lanes not paved (lab: bare lot ground); the ?gridview debug modes and the red marking of lots off the
    grid left in the code.
  - Lanes: every 6 rows, segments of 4, 72% on — by eye; they don't know the district's edge or its outer streets (a
    lane can end a cell short of a street, or run out onto the coast); two lattice directions only (long stretches can
    read straight).
  - Relaxation: 0.6 of the pull a step, 20 steps — by eye; pulls rounded to 1/64 m (the phone's 16 KB of workgroup
    memory); the apron (3 lattice cells) is shorter than 20 steps reach (a vertex at a chunk's edge a little less
    relaxed); chunks all remade when the camera enters a new one (no cache: only the new row is needed); the chunk
    table and its 9.4 KB-a-chunk scratch sized by the lot count (should be by the chunks round the camera); a joined
    house or terrace uses only its piece's four corners (its walls can sit off the cells' relaxed edges); the grid lab's
    320 cells a chunk (overflow dropped silently); phone timings not measured.
  - Heights: a cell stands on the highest ground under its corners, walls to the lowest (on a slope a cell's walls run
    down to the lowest neighbour's ground).
  - Light (archlab): the sun's direction fixed to the camera (115° round, 37° up); colours of the key, shade, bounce by
    eye; the street foot's darkening a fixed 4.5 m; the shadow map round the view redrawn every frame; windows cast no
    shadow, plants cast solid ones; depth bias by eye (speckle on the roof tiles).
  - SSAO: darkens the whole lit colour, not only the ambient; MSAA's depth read at sample 0 (halos at edges); a box
    blur blind to depth; radius 2.5 m, strength 1 by eye, in world units (its look changes with zoom); not on the
    phone, not measured there; the lab only (not the look lab, not the game).
  - Recessed openings (draw.wgsl recessAt): drawn by the wall's shader, not geometry — the silhouette doesn't change,
    the sun's shadow in a recess is worked out from the opening alone (not the shadow map); built plain windows' glass
    and doors' leaves left out so the recess shows, so a built frame whose size differs from the painted opening (a
    balcony, an attic light) keeps its own flat glass; RECESS 0.24 m.
  - AG29 first pass (open sides, jetties, steps, balconies, round towers, plants, timber/stone walls, cobbles) — its
    hacks to fix: openAt probes one point 1.4 m out per side (a narrow lane or a corner can be missed); the odds (jetty
    70%, steps 45%, balcony 60%, plants 55%, tower 20%, chimney 18%, materials 5/3/1 in 10, stone base half) and sizes
    (jetty 0.8 m, steps 0.95 m wide, balcony 0.85–0.9 m) by eye; a jetty's upper body is one quad pushed out on its
    open sides (its other sides unchanged: on a cell open on two adjacent sides the corner pushes diagonally); the
    steps and balcony ignore the windows (a balcony may cover none, steps may cross one); a tower replaces the whole
    cell (its corners left empty round it); the part budget doubled to 192 a lot (the grid's own pool instead); the
    lab ground is cobbles under every building lot (the game's ground is Track A's); interior cells (most) get
    nothing — dormers, deep eaves, steeper varied pitches, stone arches, window boxes and climbers on the grid still
    to do.
  - Roofs drain to drops (buildings round lattice points, one roof surface each) — done. Hacks to fix: roof surface
    linear across each cell from its corners (the true distance field bends inside; creases can sit off the hips);
    no eaves overhang (the roof stops flush at the walls) and no gable-end trim; slope 0.62 and cap 2.4 m by eye; hAt
    is position-only (ignores the lots' storeys, the street fronts no longer taller); an edge's openness probed at one
    point 1.4 m out; a building's cells left out by the lot rules aren't known to its siblings unless that probe finds
    them open; the chunk lot list caps at 63 (more dropped silently); the shanties' lean-tos still turn at random
    (not to a drop); a tower's radius from the nearest edge (corners of its cells left bare round it).
  - Real wall geometry (the user: "The features need real geometry on the walls ... stairs and recesses and gables";
    "overhangs to stick out ... out from the rest of the wall. That breaks up the roof lines and wall lines"): walls
    built by bay with real recesses (replaces the shader recess), projecting bays and jettied storeys that step out
    from the wall below, gables and dormers breaking the roofline.
  - Real facades, local rules, merged gabled roofs, courtyards — done. Hacks to fix: facades only on open sides
    (closed sides keep the painted windows and the shader recess); ≤ 3 bays × 4 storeys a facade (more wall above);
    every facade draws 798 vertices however small (unused ones degenerate — phone cost unmeasured); facade windows
    from the style's sizes clipped to the bay (narrow bays lose theirs); the merged roof uses its equal neighbours'
    eaves only (not theirs' neighbours': a step can show where two merged buildings see different eaves); the ridge
    direction by 5-cell block (merged buildings across a block line disagree); lot ownership by deepest point (the
    3 m reach past a parcel, 12 bits of lot id: > 4096 lots alias); a building's ground from its neighbours' owners;
    courtyard odds 13%, roof slope 0.9 and cap 4.2 m by eye; no eaves overhang, no dormers yet; towers still big
    (radius to the nearest edge).
- AG28 (the user, 2026-11-12) After the organic districts look good: "With proper cobblestones between the buildings
  and foot traffic this would read to me as an organic section of town (unplanned)" — cobbles in the lanes and
  streets, people walking. Then "The planned sections and the structures on them need work after we get these looking

- AG29 (the user, 2026-11-12, a Tiny Glade shot: "For inspiration at improving the visual fidelity and variety of the

  uses it); (2) jettied upper storeys over the lanes on brackets, deep eaves; (3) round towers with tall cones at
  corners and junctions, taller than their neighbours (cylinder and cone parts exist); (4) outside stairs to upper

  the walls' feet, window boxes, climbers, fences, crates, barrels; (6) the roofline — steeper and varied pitches,
  dormers, many chimneys, smoke later. Each from the cell's own hash; a cell's lane side found from its open
  neighbours (also AG27's street fronts).
- AG25 (the user, 2026-11-12) Synty trees on the phone as pre-rendered sprites (4–8 views, colour + normal + depth),

  the phone are "a single smeared low res window on a solid color" against detailed roofs — baking at PC detail answers
  it on the phone.
- AG26 (the user, 2026-11-12, a Townscaper shot: "Lets target at least this quality in our toy test") Not flat:
  - Light: Townscaper's warm key / cool teal shade — done 7316a78e; the sun's cast shadows in archlab — done (this
    commit); screen-space occlusion — done (archlab, ssao.wgsl); next edge lines (creases dark, edges
    caught by the light).
  - The grid relaxed (Townscaper's squaring) in seamless GPU chunks — done (this commit). Next: curved walls along
    each built group's outline ("spline blending along adjacent cells that merge into walls").
  - Whole cells, the cells a road crosses and the ground between blocks paved ("make those 0 blocks but have the
    ground between blocks pave fully") — done (this commit).
  - The grid alone first (the user: "A streamable infinite irregular grid with no holes and well shaped cells"; "decouple
    the lot budget from the irregular grid entirely"): the grid lab (?gridlab=1) and a second merge round — done (this
    commit). Then lots and roads on it (the user's first guess: a cell crossed by a lot it isn't part of left empty so
    the street shows, the streets paved under), its parts out of the lots' budget.
  - Lots on the grid — done (this commit): a cell stands if all its corners are in grid lots and out of the streets;
    straddling lots fine; slivers between lots go to the nearest lot; a cell on the highest ground, walls to the lowest.
    Next: the streets paved under; parts out of the lots' budget (a per-chunk pool); lotAt by a per-chunk lot index.
  - Districts much bigger relative to cells (the user: "a district should be much bigger relative to cells so that we
    get a large neighborhood of the irregular structures"); and for adjacent lots of the same type, the road between
    them replaced by one aligned to the grid (the user's idea) — citygen is Track A's: to agree with them.
  - Grid districts grow over lot lines, their own lanes along the grid's edges — done (this commit).
  - Wall detail (the user, 2026-11-12: "Windows and doors are not recessed", "there are not many overhangs or porches")
    — with the geometry list below.
  - Smoothed normals between nearly parallel neighbouring grid cells ("Townscaper also looks like it renders with
    smoothed normals between nearly parallel irregular grid cells"): adjacent cells' walls that meet almost flat shade
    as one curved wall.
  - Geometry ("there is more geometry. Rounded corners, set back windows, balconies and arches"): corners rounded or
    bevelled, windows recessed with sills and lintels, balconies (slab, brackets, railing in the trim colour, pots),
    arches through ground floors and arcades.
  - Variety ("you never added the corner stone features to the architecture. Or any of the other variety like plants
    or patches of brick showing through plaster"): quoins on plaster and brick corners, brick showing through where
    plaster has fallen, plants on buildings (window boxes, climbers, pots; ivy exists on some lots only).
  - The phone gets all of it ("No put that detail on phone please. I think it's less of an issue than the curved
    rooftops"): the curved tile roofs are what the phone gives up if it must, not the new geometry.
- AG19 ("working with better ground and building textures") Texture sets (albedo/normal/roughness/AO) for walls, roofs,
  ground: ~12–16 CC0 sets (Poly Haven / ambientCG) in one texture array, KTX2-compressed, 512 phone / 1k PC; the
  generator keeps per-part tint, wear and pick. Waits on the user: may CC0 textures go in the public repo?
